SQL/JOINs/complex queries/persistent storage exceeding several GB/advanced analysis are excluded from the data-foundation requirements; when needed, hand raw data to the user for their own analysis instead. Data-integrity checks are ported to the Kernel side, and search is handled as read-only computation over files/manifests on R2. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-FND-03 | Design the system as a "file-based research data lake that protects an individual's life history and re-analyzability." Since R2 cannot be searched by directory structure alone, a Parquet manifest is kept as an index table, and search reads that manifest via DuckDB/Polars. While the entry count can be strongly narrowed down within roughly 1,000–10,000, cosine computation over the subset suffices without FAISS (FAISS is a speed-up component, not a foundational philosophy); low latency is not a requirement, and re-analyzability is prioritized instead. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-FND-04 | World-state updates occur only through a fully separated, one-way pipeline: Command → pure-function Reducer (Kernel) → storage save. The Kernel is deterministic (same input → same output) and has zero side effects, holding no IO/DB access/signature verification/EventBus/Scheduler/DSL evaluation/CRDT. Conflict detection uses OCC (prevSnapshotVersion must match), timestamp monotonicity is guaranteed (command.timestamp > world.lastTimestamp), and deletion of record/root nodes is prohibited. Integrity is guaranteed by the Kernel, not by DB constraints (FK/CHECK/UNIQUE/TRIGGER). | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-FND-05 | Civilization identity is defined by a hash chain of the Genesis Hash plus a continuous sequence of R2 events (each event fixes prev_hash/event_hash = SHA-256); continuing to copy R2 is a branch of the same civilization, initializing R2 is a new civilization, and fork_point_hash makes the branch explicit. World state is uniquely represented by snapshotVersion = worldHash (a Merkle aggregation of all node hashes), used for tamper detection and integrity verification. | Constraint | Medium | Required | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-FND-06 | World state is stored as an immutable snapshot; instead of overwriting, versions are appended to preserve history. snapshot-XXXX.json may never be overwritten; only latest.json may receive pointer updates. Derived layers such as embeddings are versioned via generation-N/immutable manifests, with latest updated only via pointer, keeping old generations for history so that a past worldSnapshot can be reproduced with its corresponding embedding. The diff between snapshots is computed and visualized as node additions/deletions/changes (props/links/meta) and changes to rootIds. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-FND-12 | Fix the dependency direction as one-way, apps → libs/ihl\|packages\|components (cross-app imports, libs → apps, libs/shared/, and cross-feature deep imports are all prohibited); on the frontend, fix the one-way direction UI → Layout → Component → FeatureNode → Kernel → R2. Limit tree depth (docs 4 / feature sub 3 / scripts 3 / libs domain 2 / apps and packages 1 each) and prohibit nested npm packages. The authoritative schema source is `02-設計/_横断/schema/` (the root `schemas/` directory is sync-only; the reverse direction is codegen). Avoid a bloated main.py by domain-packaging it, keep API routes thin, and colocate the Web app by feature unit (a rule of thumb of 15 files or fewer per directory). Each FeatureNode gets an independent R2 key space and can be added loosely coupled, without bumping schema_version. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-FND-14 | The minimum unit for the system's synchronization, connection, and management is C-USB (Civilization-USB: core/rag/io/compatibility/security, IN → Transform → OUT), and no new proprietary format that doesn't presuppose this may be created. Connect every Component so it is swappable, traceable, and semantically compatible; express every entity via CoreEntityBase (lineage metadata `core` + search metadata `rag`) so it is RAG-searchable (title/tags/embedding). OSS is thinly wrapped, and what gets fixed is limited to the schema/manifest/ID/input-output contract (manifest.yaml + unit/contract tests + per-component CI; the anti-pattern of the UI calling OSS directly is prohibited). When swapping components, verify six items — interface match / dependency compatibility / screen context / AI resources / RAG / style — with a green/yellow/red rating. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-FND-15 | Attach lineage metadata (uuid/parent_uuid/ancestor_chain/lineage_hash/content_hash/semantic_hash/generation) uniformly to every data structure capable of evolving, enabling tracking and tamper detection as points (UUID), lines (lineage), and planes (civilization). All IDs follow a unified ULID/UUID convention (prefixes such as ind_/cap_/run_/snap_/emb_, or a prefix-less UUID managed separately by species tag); timestamps are UTC ISO8601, hashes are SHA256, null/empty string/undefined are prohibited (fields with no value are omitted), and JSON configuration files use UTF-8 without a BOM. Every derived artifact must carry reproducibility metadata (run_id/schema_version/pipeline_version/model_version/input_hash/created_at). There is also a proposal to build on a ProjectId/unified entity schema (id/type/qid/tags/attributes/relations) as the backbone. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-FND-16 | Approve the premise of a fork culture in which the civilization remains the same civilization even if the entire configuration is replaced (R2 = the sacred domain and cannot be forked). However, two points — (a) whether the Kernel can be forked, and (b) the unit of OS replacement (per user, or one per civilization) — remain undecided by the stakeholder, so a decision is deferred and will be reflected in this entry once determined. | Constraint | High | Required | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-FND-17 | it-hercules-laboratory is the sole authoritative source for the new product (public OSS); civilization-os is restricted to reference/salvage as legacy/archive only (porting R2 I/O philosophy, requirement wording, schema column proposals, lineage UI culture), and new-feature implementation on the frontend/backend or bidirectional mirror synchronization is prohibited. OSS is published as a single clone of a full repo unifying design docs 01–05 with apps/libs (the two-product model of data lake + UI is discontinued). Existing usable code is borrowed into a new folder and rebuilt cleanly (salvage means keeping the wiring — api/hook/types — while rebuilding the look, CSS/JSX, per Option A), building on top of existing assets (Truth/GitHub authoritative source + R2 mirror/CiteRef) rather than creating a new parallel structure. The wiki's authoritative source is git. External APIs, naming conventions, layer structure, and the component system are fixed before OSS release, and Galápagos-style divergence is prohibited. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| 【Pending — see Chapter 7】 V3-FND-24 | (Old DB exploration, superseded) A proposal to use a minimal three-table physical layer of universe_event (world event log) + state (generic JSONB state, UNIQUE(entity_type, entity_id, key)) + auth_user, letting new features be realized purely by adding UI schema without creating app-specific tables. Alternatively, a proposal to adopt Postgres (Neon)/Cloudflare D1, splitting into schemas such as core/derived/market/raw/semantic/universe, with major tables completely prohibiting UPDATE/DELETE via a forbid_mutation trigger (immutable), freezing schema.json, and prohibiting breaking changes. A generic node system (paper/entity_data/failure/complaint/article/product, etc.) was also explored. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-GOV-09 | When a government or authority raises an issue, immediately set a "do-not-use flag" on the relevant listing/data/image (R2 is not DELETEd) and impose Δcount +10 on the target user. The developer does not act as judge but does comply with administrative orders (human gate). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-GOV-22 | Classify features/UI/parts/code into four layers (3: feature composition / 2: UI composition / 1: parts / 0: code) plus fixed-asset layer 4, and define fork/vote/like/favorite eligibility per layer. Fixed assets (brand assets, worldview settings, Hercules individual information) are immutable assets that cannot be voted on or forked. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-GOV-25 | Change Management Protocol (CMP) / AI Administrator (A90) constitution: every spec change must go through "documenting the reason for change → updating the spec (engineer/human/ai) → posting to the board → GitHub commit → linking post_id ↔ commit_sha ↔ R2 history." Changes whose reason (rationale, scope of impact, inconsistencies, civilizational intent, benefit) is ambiguous are prohibited. The seven extended-spec elements (Intent Schema / Change Conflict Map / Health Check / Self-Documentation / Future Prediction / Worldview Guardian / F-Law) serve as the quality gate. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-I18-15 | Per-country translation (A12) targets only "UI expressions" — UI wording, button labels, tags, category names, etc. — for override; system error text, world law, user post bodies, DMs, marketplace listing titles, paper bodies, and species/morph names are prohibited from being overridden (translation/{country}/{key}.json). | Constraint | Medium | Required | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-IND-02 | The storage location for the individual master is the individual table in IHL R2 as the authoritative source; civilization-os only references individual_id. Tables such as individuals/papers are not "facts" but identification scaffolding that receives observations, and they do not hold the substance of growth data, etc. Individuals are consistently identified with a UID (QR) from larva through pupa, adult, and specimen, and all images, values, and environment logs are tied to that UID. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-IND-08 | The matchmaking app's scoring engine has O(number of tags) computational complexity, requires no GPU, and requires no deep learning/black-box inference; evaluation logs prohibit DELETE, so the ideal vector can be fully reconstructed from history even after a learning-rate change or formula change. Accuracy is evaluated with Precision@K / AUC (valid at 0.7 or above) / score separation / vector change magnitude / learning stability index, and convergence is judged accordingly. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Not Implemented |
| V3-IND-17 | Individual observation data records death and failure too as formal statuses (alive/dead/failed, fail_stage, fail_reason), prohibits deletion/editing, and saves it to R2 via INSERT ONLY, eliminating survivorship bias (a lost individual is still part of the lineage's truth; pruning for tidiness is not research). Lineage statistics formally include failure-rate metrics such as survival rate, hatch rate, mortality at larva/prepupa/pupa/adult, sex ratio, and color reproducibility. Failure data (beginners' mistakes, traps, accidents, deaths) is recorded as "antifragile data" — more valuable than success — in a FailureDataNode (cause, environmental conditions, impact, recovery method), so future beginners don't repeat the same failures. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-MKT-40 | The market ledger is structured as double-entry bookkeeping (Σdebit = Σcredit, non-negative balances, immutable), treating offer/ownership/trade as append-only immutable logs. Transaction execution prevents double-execution via a UNIQUE constraint on idempotency_key; coin increases/decreases are system-role only and event-recorded, and state is an aggregation of events, so a structure where any event/state mismatch immediately reveals fraud (the rule lives on the API side, not in DB triggers). | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-OBS-05 | Observations are append-only, with an editing UI prohibited; corrections are expressed as a new append (APPEND). UPDATE, DELETE, and tampering with R2/Truth are entirely prohibited, and even AI analysis results are re-registered as a new Observation rather than updating an existing one. Generated/display layers (Graph/Cache/Index) are regenerable via ETL and must not hold facts. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-OBS-06 | Every measured/feature value must be accompanied by a value_origin (direct_observed/image_derived/environment_derived/lineage_derived/estimated/imputed/aggregate/unknown), so that values with the same name but different origins are never conflated. Interpolation is distinguished as imputed and substitute estimation as estimated. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-OBS-15 | Environmental data stores only raw values (temperature, humidity, light_level); recomputable derived values such as dew point, vapor pressure deficit, and absolute humidity are not kept in Truth and are instead recomputed at query time (minimal storage, determinism first — replacing the formula later never corrupts past data). Environment acquisition is also append-only, with corrections made as new records. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-OBS-52 | RawData/original images such as photos, audio, and raw sensor data are not put into the RDB but stored in R2/S3, tied 1:N to Observation; the data body is never discarded, and meaning is not attached to it. Observation images are always saved, and if templates/AI/technology improve, they are re-measured and tagged with a "re-measurement tag." RAW observations are kept as an append-only log. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-PPR-09 | Every derived artifact carries run_id, model_name/version, input_hash, schema_version, and provenance, enabling reproducibility tracing. Every measured value carries a value_origin (direct_observed/image_derived/environment_derived/estimated), every measurement row carries a source enum, and observed_at is kept separate from committed_at. Tier B environmental gaps have imputed values separated out, and bundle v1, scale sheets, and a reanalysis-manifest (an aggregation of fact keys such as imageR2Key/scaleTemplateId/bpcmsEngineProfile, excluding image binaries) are provided so a third party can re-run under the same conditions. clientContentDigest (canonical SHA-256) is carried in the commit body (not yet wired in ver1). | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-PPR-10 | Freeze the analysis engine with semantic versioning (Major = fixed per paper / Minor = compatibility guaranteed / Patch = bug fixes only, e.g. v1.x = CIE76, v2.x = CIEDE2000), allowing papers to state the engine version explicitly (e.g. BPCMS Engine v1.2.0). Freeze BPCMS v1.0, the standard for reproducibly quantifying adult beetle body-surface color (gray-card correction, automatic 90% ROI, ΔE (CIE76), D2 (within-individual color unevenness σ), 32-bin histogram, quality_score); analyses are never overwritten but saved as separate records (1 individual : N analyses), and a re-analysis log (old-vs-new diff) is recorded. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 2 | Not Implemented |
| V3-SEC-24 | AI must never autonomously change the authoritative text of the Terms of Service or legal wording (#02 HUMAN-02-LEGAL: article text immutable, human gate). Before publication, a human (expert) review per the human-review-checklist is performed and evidence retained. Entering the operator name, contact, governing law, and jurisdiction is a human gate. Legal translation of the ToS is also a human gate, and a machine-translated ToS body is never treated as authoritative (a link to the authoritative-language version is shown). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-SEC-42 | Attach SHA-256 (original image, ROI mask, analysis-result JSON) and a Merkle-style worldHash/nodeHash to image and analysis data, used for tamper detection, transfer-corruption detection, R2 data integrity, authenticity proof at paper submission, and guaranteeing "this image was captured by this system." No external image-upload path (other than observation/image) is created, and official observations are limited to kind:image.capture only. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-46 | Logic is fixed in the API, and the UI remains merely a "skin" that calls the API. All valuable operations (coin increase/decrease, ratings, usage counts) are controlled by the API as the rules of the world; dangerous operations are kept off the API whitelist, preserving a structure where the world (DB) cannot break even if the UI breaks. The DB stays a thin recording device, and logic in DB triggers is avoided due to loss of transparency. External connections go only to destinations permitted by the API whitelist (including when the OS is forked). | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-WIK-06 | Every Topic page must have a Citations section referencing Truth events, design docs, and the dictionary; claims without a source are never written, and cited paths must actually exist. As a mandatory condition immediately after distillation completes, a deterministic check — verifying citations actually exist and mechanically cross-checking them against the index entry and file content — is positioned as required (not relying solely on a monthly batch). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-WIK-09 | Distill only stable, high-confidence knowledge into the wiki layer; high-frequency-update, large-volume data (raw time-series images, numeric logs, etc.) stays in the Truth layer and is searched on demand — this two-layer hybrid boundary is codified explicitly. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-WIK-10 | Writes to the knowledge layer are limited to going through a single process, avoiding concurrent write conflicts from multiple agents/multiple users. | Constraint | Medium | — | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-WIK-12 | Knowledge-layer distillation demonstrations never mix fake sources into a real bundle (synthetic events are used only in a temporary bundle to demonstrate the mechanism). | Constraint | Medium | — | Out of Scope | Unknown |

#### 5.2.2 Authentication Boundaries & Authorization (12 entries)

| ID | Requirement Text | Type | trust | Human Confirm | wave | ver2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V3-AUT-01 | Authentication is via email magic link only (passwordless); passwords, OAuth/OIDC, SMS, TOTP, and social/SNS/phone login are intentionally unsupported. The flow is fixed to a single path: "enter email → send link → verify token → establish session." | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-AUT-02 | The magic-link token has a 15-minute TTL and is one-time-use (once verified, it is updated to consumed in R2); wherever a variable string such as a token is placed in a URL, it must always be URL-encoded (an actual bug occurred where verification failed due to missing encoding). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-AUT-04 | Production SMTP keys and sender-domain configuration for the magic link are a human gate; actual email sending is done from the VPS (SMTP must never be completed solely by Workers), and the link destination is fixed to the production frontend via PUBLIC_APP_URL. Real values for SMTP/PUBLIC_APP_URL/JWT_SECRET/R2 are supplied via environment variables, and the send → click → session-established E2E flow is verified. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Not Implemented |
| V3-AUT-11 | Explicitly define the authentication → initial-setup flow (registration → country/language → terms of service → home), and manage all screen transitions as navigation.json (missing transition specs are a source of AI misinference). | Constraint | Medium | Required | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-AUT-13 | The authentication boundary defaults to deny (when in doubt, protect); only the authentication entry points (/login, /register), terms (/terms), language (/language), and observation READ (Scope A) are public, and every unenumerated route is redirected to /login with 307 via a middleware fallback. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-AUT-14 | Under observation, READ is the default and WRITE is a deny-list of enumerated routes; a new WRITE route is added only by appending it to OBSERVATION_WRITE_PREFIXES (an omission from the enumeration is an exposure incident). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-AUT-16 | The observation search scope is Scope A (community); logged-in users can search and browse the entire observation catalog across all users, with no narrowing by owner_user_id/session actor. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-AUT-17 | Writes and personal settings (commit, naming, individuals, devices, etc.) are scoped to the individual user via useActorId()/session actor_id. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-AUT-23 | The initial grant of admin privileges is done once, via the shortest method of writing directly to adminUserId in world.json, and no permanent grant UI is left in place. | Constraint | Medium | Required | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| 【Pending — see Chapter 7】 V3-AUT-25 | Treat permissions as a "law of physics": WorldNode carries owner and permissions{read,write,delete}, and the Kernel (a pure-function reducer) enforces verification on every CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE. If permissions is undefined, the world defaults to open (all permitted). | Constraint | Medium | Required | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| 【Pending — see Chapter 7】 V3-AUT-42 | The account system for the new IHL repository is TBD; at public release, a separate authentication scheme and separate ToS are assumed, so civilization-os's magic-link registration is not implemented as-is in IHL (or it replaces the old prototype as open registration). | Constraint | High | Required | Wave 2 | Not Implemented |
| V3-AUT-45 | The three legacy register/locale/complete-onboarding APIs are not reverted back to a multi-step UI; the sole current canonical path is setup-profile (regression prevention). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |

#### 5.2.3 PII & Personal Information Protection (17 entries)

| ID | Requirement Text | Type | trust | Human Confirm | wave | ver2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V3-AUT-03 | Sessions use an opaque session_token, resolved via server-side verification per the ver4-infra-agreement, as the ver3 standard, replacing the old JWT (localStorage-held) approach. Tokens avoid placing a plaintext JWT directly in localStorage, and expiration/revocation management is held server-side. | Constraint | High | Required | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-AUT-18 | Individual QR codes are an app-scheme deep link at ihl://individual/<id>, with no public web destination reachable by an anonymous browser (no public read page is created). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-AUT-30 | On account deletion, observation data (especially data already cited in papers) is retained append-only, while personal information (PII such as email address) is masked/separated for protection, by design. The explicit wording in the terms of service and legal-compliance judgment (e.g. GDPR) go through a legal gate (human); design work may proceed ahead of it, but finalizing the terms' text is done by a human. | Constraint | Medium | Required | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-BBS-09 | The board's poster-name input field is removed, unified into a readonly display of the fixed username ({{currentUser.username}}/civilization ID) determined at initial registration (anonymous-by-default is discontinued). | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-IND-30 | Research individual IDs and observation IDs are treated as "research IDs, not biological/personal information" and kept separate from personal information; the individual ID (bioId) is never masked (data transparency is prioritized above all, so data used in papers can be traced back to the individual; personal information such as email is masked). Consent to provide data is obtained through the terms of service. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-MKT-28 | Personal information related to transactions (name, shipping address, bank account, post-office-hold settings) is never entered on the board; it is registered on a settings screen before the transaction and is viewable only by the transaction counterparty. Transaction-time PII is held encrypted only during the transaction and either erased once the transaction completes, or delegated to GMO/the shipping carrier so that IHL itself never retains it. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-OBS-54 | Observation data is tamper-proof, transparent, and reproducible; fabrication, theft of others' observations, and AI forgery are prohibited, numerical alteration is prohibited, and only interpretation may be appended with a timestamp. Personal information is anonymized before publication, and observation data cannot be placed into protected_assets, always remaining as a video-generation candidate/civilization asset (though the user may explicitly opt to hide it). Data has three-tier disclosure (viewable by anyone / tied to the individual UID / judgment logs non-public). | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-01 | Observation data is, in principle, open data (with a selectable license), and paper bodies, with copyright considerations, publish only metadata and a structured summary. Personal information is either not collected or anonymization is mandatory, and contributor consent and terms of service are made explicit. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-04 | Secret values (.env/credentials/API keys/Cloudflare/Sakura VPS credentials/SwitchBot/R2 keys/Webhook secrets) are never written into the repository, doc bodies, R2, the frontend, or logs; they are injected at runtime via Docker's env_file. Before every commit, a final scan is done via git status/git log -p, and secrets appear only as placeholders/variable names. Plaintext logging of secrets is prohibited, and a Webhook signature mismatch returns 401 (without logging the secret, returning only a short reason). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-06 | The system holds no personal or payment information whatsoever (name/address/phone/credit card/bank account). Payment (fund transfer) is left to bank transfers between users, and the OS is not involved; a fixed percentage of sales (e.g. 8%) is accrued when a transaction completes, reconciled and deducted using a unique remittance-name code per user. Identity linking is limited to external integrations such as X/Facebook and never brings personal information in (there is nothing to protect, so the security burden is inherently low). | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-07 | Before saving, personal information is always processed in the order PII detection → masking → save, and saving, restoring, or leaving pre-mask data in logs is entirely prohibited. Truth/R2 never holds plaintext PII (name, address, phone, bank account); user identification uses only user_id_hash, and email_index is kept as a SHA256 hash key. Collected observation PII is never saved to Truth in plaintext; it is either encrypted or held temporarily within the session only. PII (name/email/phone/address/SNS ID/credentials/.env values/location) is detected across the document set and masked with {{PII:TYPE}}, generating a candidate list for the human gate, a clean dataset, and a diff report, while the original is kept unmodified and quarantined. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-09 | The PII access audit log (pii_access_event) records only metadata — who accessed what, when — never the PII body itself. PII deletion requests are anonymized immediately, with no 90-day retention grace period. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-11 | The profile holds no address; identity is only UUID/UserID/display name, and the OS holds no address database. Shipping agreements are made through a channel between the parties, and anonymous shipping / post-office holds are recommended (reducing the risk of a well-known breeder's address being exposed, and ensuring temperature-stable receipt of live specimens). Locating a post office uses an external official search (post-office-hold-hint). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-25 | Prepare legal drafts of the terms of service, privacy policy, and marketplace/platinum-coin usage notices, along with a human-confirmation checklist, under docs/legal/, explicitly stating that these are not legal advice. Compliance with Japanese law (the Act on the Protection of Personal Information, the Telecommunications Business Act, etc.) is treated as design memos, without asserting legality, and presupposes practical/attorney confirmation. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-29 | The terms of service explicitly list the items collected (email address, country, language, behavior history/screen transitions/AI usage history) and state that they are not used for advertising purposes. Behavior logs (screen transitions, post history, AI usage history) are used only for service improvement and fraud prevention. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-34 | Establish a permission layer (P0) allowing external data ingestion only via share buttons/authenticated APIs/OAuth (spaces the user owns, such as GitHub/Notion/Google Drive/YouTube), and entirely prohibit scraping, automated fetching of login-required pages, paid articles, others' SNS/DMs/personal information, robots.txt-forbidden sites, and unpermitted domains. Other people's accounts/workspaces are never fetched (merely handing over a URL must never let third-party data be loaded into the AI without consent). | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-40 | Instruct the AI summarization prompt to remove personal information (such as email addresses), and fix the summary language to Japanese. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Out of Scope | Not Implemented |

#### 5.2.4 Security & Legal (Excluding PII) (28 entries — plus 1 rejected entry noted at the end of §5.2)

| ID | Requirement Text | Type | trust | Human Confirm | wave | ver2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V3-SEC-02 | The collector agent's private key is held only in collector/.env, and env ingest from the local collector is Ed25519-signed. The public key is auto-derived from the private key and synced to the API side's .env via a script, eliminating key-configuration mistakes from manual copy-paste. The design avoids a server-side poller (a path that places the token in the backend .env), preventing the operational error of placing secrets on the server. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-03 | External service API keys/secrets such as SwitchBot's are never held or used on the server side; they are managed only on the user's side (Docker/.env/browser localStorage). The web version supports manual entry/CSV import only; users with Docker set up can fetch data — this is aimed at power users. In the future, each user will be able to add and manage their own keys from a GUI, fetching data in a single batch at the same time as photo capture to prevent time drift. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Carried over from ver2 |
| V3-SEC-05 | An LLM API key is sent only when saving, is never returned in a response, and only an existence flag is displayed. The paper LLM and individual-observation LLM are kept as independent fields and never mixed. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-08 | The user ID in public data is irreversibly hashed as public_user_id = SHA256(user_id + secret_salt), making the original ID unrecoverable while still allowing continuous tracking of the same user (masking, not deletion). | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Not Implemented |
| V3-SEC-10 | For sandbox use, user IDs are reassigned as sequential numbers 1, 2, 3, ...; the mapping table (routing table) is held only in local encrypted storage and never saved to R2. This safely provides external developers with a verification environment in a full clone. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-14 | Rate limits are imposed on login-related endpoints (magiclink: 20 requests/60 seconds/IP; verify: 60 requests/60 seconds/IP). | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-15 | As an open-redirect guard, an authenticated /login?next= permits only internal absolute paths (starting with / and not // ), and external URLs are dropped to /. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-16 | Never assume a security mechanism (such as an authentication gate) "works just because it was added"; confirm with an e2e test that unauthenticated access is actually rejected (a lesson learned from middleware becoming ineffective when not placed directly under src/). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-17 | Authentication on the image-fetch endpoint is never excluded (exempted) unless the blob-fetch approach proves insufficient. Observation images are fetched via a blob-authenticated fetch when IHL_AUTH_REQUIRED=1. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-19 | A human confirms production environment variables — the VPS API must have IHL_AUTH_REQUIRED=1 with IHL_AUTH_BYPASS unset, and Pages Web must have IHL_WEB_AUTH_BYPASS unset (if set to 1, every route passes without authentication). | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-26 | The division of responsibility between the self-hosted edition and the cloud-provided edition is explicitly stated in the terms, consistently stating in the ToS/Privacy Policy that "the data controller is the self-hosting user themselves." | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-SEC-27 | IHL creates a new, independent ToS/Privacy Policy on the it-hercules-laboratory side, separate from civilization-os, and does not reuse civ-os's terms. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 2 | Not Implemented |
| V3-SEC-28 | Article 1 of the terms of service explicitly states that "these terms may change (due to solo development, keeping pace with the times, and improving on gaps)." | Constraint | Medium | — | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-35 | The system is not used to retrain external AI models; ingested data is used only for inference, storage, tagging, and script generation — a structure that avoids the gray areas of future AI-training regulation law, copyright, and privacy. Input data for video generation, etc. is made explicit as not including AI-training-prohibited data, and its source is made verifiable. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-36 | While API data use for research purposes is made freely available, a policy of prohibiting commercial use to prevent fraud and competition is under consideration. | Constraint | Medium | — | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-37 | Template/OSS ingestion selects only openly licensed ones for batch ingestion, and license input (type/source URL/credit requirement/redistribution permission) is mandatory at ingestion time, with OS behavior controlled per license (CC-BY auto-inserts and locks the credit, redistribution-NG blocks publication, GPL is rejected at ingestion). Copyright issues are avoided by combining OSS parts to decorate the UI rather than importing finished code. Any OSS used has its license (commercial restrictions, etc.) checked in advance. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-38 | Link previews are self-hosted and do not depend on external oEmbed (consistent with observation blob authentication). | Constraint | High | — | Out of Scope | Not Implemented |
| V3-SEC-39 | Content bodies are Markdown, with an XSS filter applied so that only sanitized HTML is rendered, and raw HTML injection is prohibited. Client-side translation minimizes sending data to third-party SaaS, and when an external API is used, explicit consent and the data path are aligned with the ToS/Privacy Policy, and translation output is never made an XSS entry point. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 2 | Not Implemented |
| V3-SEC-41 | ValueCheck/preference sessions are used only for a search boost combined with the individual's own JWT, and are never leaked to other users. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-SEC-45 | User code/driver execution runs fully isolated within a sandbox boundary (Extism/Docker/CUSB/WebAssembly/vm), with the production DB read-only, the test DB destructible, no external communication, and CPU/memory limits imposed. A failure returns 400. No-code users get GrapesJS+Blockly, advanced users get Monaco+JSON, and developers create a Fork on GitHub; fork-execution safety is guaranteed by a Whitelist (Component/API/Workflow) plus Sandbox plus permission control. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-47 | Value-changing operations are enforced server-side: coin increases/decreases are server-only (role=system), ratings are limited to once per user via UNIQUE(user_id, target), usage counts are rate-limited, and user_id is force-assigned server-side. Fraud prevention includes prohibiting individual DM-based transactions (transactions are limited to within the platform), IP/device fingerprinting, and imposing a karma decrease for abnormal behavior. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-48 | Data read from storage and JSON received via the API must always pass structural validation before being handed to the Kernel, defending against corrupted data contaminating the laws of physics (the Kernel). The Kernel enforces permissions as a law of physics on every operation (CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE) — owner mismatches are prohibited, and write permission is also verified on referenced targets — and Universe (public/internal/market) is physically separated (by directory) to guarantee the scope of publication. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-50 | A user's brand assets (logos, etc.) are registered as "prohibited/protected assets" (A110/protected_assets.json), providing an NG-settings screen that lets usage in video generation, UI generation, and RAG search be individually toggled on/off. The asset type (brand/general/observation) is explicitly specified by the user, never auto-determined (not-adopted ≠ BAN, avoiding a mistaken BAN with no penalty). Observation data (a civilization asset) cannot be placed into protected assets and is always treated as a candidate for civilization observation/adoption. Only whether AI training is permitted can be chosen separately via a toggle. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-51 | It is mandatory that published/shared templates (world blueprints) never include individual data or observation logs — a required safety design. | Constraint | Medium | — | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-52 | A mechanism that fetches information on its own via cron etc. while the user is absent is avoided, since it may pose a problem under Japanese law; instead, the API is called when the user opens the web app (an active action). If a server-side cron does auto-fetch, an explicit user-consent UI must always be shown in advance. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-SEC-53 | Per-user memory (/users/{user_id}/memory/) and civilization-common memory (/civilization/memory/) are separated into two layers; when the AI responds, personal data is searched with top priority and civilization-common data as a supplement. An individual's learning data never mixes with other users'. | Constraint | Medium | — | Experimental | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-54 | Initial data (with user-related information masked) can be distributed to those who request it, but the timing of distribution is handled cautiously to avoid proliferation/imitation while the system is still immature. | Constraint | Medium | — | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-56 | Listing-state writes, template publication, GMO, etc. are protected by authorization (requireMarketListingStateWrite, etc.), but the fact that POST /listing-registry lacks authentication is explicitly flagged as a known security gap and a candidate for improvement. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |

#### 5.2.5 Reproducibility & Provenance (provenance / replay / determinism) (6 entries)

| ID | Requirement Text | Type | trust | Human Confirm | wave | ver2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V3-AIP-04 | Independence of adversarial verification is ensured by always inserting a deterministic check (lint/test/schema) upfront, and by running the critic as a role-separated, independent agent. A cap is set on the number of back-and-forth rounds; when the cap is reached, the planning side finishes it via a fallback, and the full back-and-forth log is recorded in markdown. Whether to also use a different vendor's model is a human decision, since it is a policy shift. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-AIP-83 | Maintain a numbering convention that systematizes screens/features into number bands (A-series: 0-9 basics / 20-29 marketplace / 30-39 research / 40-49 board / 50 world law / 60-99 culture/AI, etc.), unifying each feature module under a common directory structure of api/logic/ui/page_info/threads, producing a reproducible layout with no ambiguity for implementation, testing, or AI auto-generation. | Constraint | Medium | — | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-CST-05 | Deployment/operations procedures are established: HTTPS is published via nginx+certbot (Let's Encrypt) with automatic certificate renewal, pm2 keeps the process resident/restarting/auto-starting, and a human manually confirms deploy/nginx/ihl-api.conf is not overwritten whenever certbot/nginx is updated. A release follows three steps: backend (pull → install → build → restart) → frontend (pull → install → build → deploy) → apply config/env. When rebuilding the VPS, 2 GB swap, nginx+certbot, R2 key reuse, etc. are reproduced from a copy-paste-level Japanese runbook. Production R2 is not migrated from the dev environment; it starts empty or with seed data. Local development is unified on Docker (docker-compose up only). .env is split into two tiers: admin/infrastructure-only (.env.platform) and user-configurable (.env.local, SwitchBot, etc.). GMO production deposits and the ver4 production cutover (DNS switch/rollback) are human gates. | Constraint | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
| V3-OBS-10 | Similarity search proceeds via a determinism-first ladder (metadata whitelist narrowing → embedding cosine on a subset narrowed by size/color/lineage/qc → weighted rerank for top-K), and never performs a full scan. Table search reading a Parquet manifest via DuckDB/Polars is the normal search; Phase 1 requires no FAISS/resident DB (subset numpy cosine handles up to roughly 10,000 entries), and FAISS (IndexFlatIP → IVF/HNSW/PQ) plus id_map plus build_info are introduced to R2 only once the entry count grows. Inputs are broadened to ideal reference images/viewpoint vectors and text embeddings, also covering bimodal/prototype (average vector of a selected group) search. Phase 1 does not require low latency. The query itself is excluded from results. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |
| V3-OTH-12 | Guarantee reproducibility of generated output: the same input must always return the same result, and non-deterministic processing such as random numbers is prohibited (World Law v1.1, Article 2, the Law of Reproducibility). | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-WIK-03 | Search uses a deterministic ladder (keyword extraction → score via index.md without opening files → open only the single best file → read only the relevant section → one model call at the end), concentrating the model call into a single distillation step. Text-embedding search is a second stage, defaulting to a dummy deterministic backend, with the production backend to be chosen by a human at a later date. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 1 | Partial |

#### 5.2.6 Availability & Fault Tolerance (6 entries)

| ID | Requirement Text | Type | trust | Human Confirm | wave | ver2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V3-AIP-66 | Git operating rules: never commit unless the user explicitly instructs it; never force-push or make persistent changes to git config; resolve non-fast-forwards via rebase; split work logs into multiple commits by logical unit; always do a full commit before applying a spec change so it can be easily rolled back on GitHub; and confirm the app runs locally before pushing. | Constraint | Medium | — | Wave 1 | Unknown |
| V3-AUT-32 | As an account-takeover countermeasure, weak security questions are not adopted; identity is instead assured via a backup email, device trust, login history, and abnormal-login warnings, with a two-step confirmation added only for critical operations such as email changes, deletion, and large transactions. Whether to introduce TOTP/2FA as opt-in is decided separately, after confirming consistency with V3-AUT-01. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | Required | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-CST-03 | Optimize AI operating cost: investigation tasks are run on a lower-tier model rather than a top-tier model directly, and a three-tier model-routing scheme routes low-judgment-density work (exploration/grep/simple lookups) to the lowest-tier model (e.g. Haiku). The subagent-delegation template must explicitly state the token budget, retry cap, and failure-escalation condition; exploratory delegation is kept to a single level, never spawning grandchild agents. Each PLAN document adds a one-line estimated token budget. Work on the billing specifics of headless execution (claude -p) begins only after primary-source confirmation. For large document sets, tokens are saved via NotebookLM summarization delegation, diff/table-of-contents-first reading, preferring existing references, and distributing reading across subagents. There is concern about cost being borne solely by Auto mode. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 2 | Unknown |
| V3-I18-12 | On-device execution of translation AI (with the user bearing the compute cost) is the first choice; if device performance is insufficient, it falls back to server-side translation to guarantee quality. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | Required | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-SEC-49 | Maintain a self-defense mechanism (Self-Protect) that autonomously detects and rejects dangerous diffs (breaking changes) and, if necessary, automatically rolls the OS back to a past version. | Non-Functional Requirement | Medium | — | Out of Scope | Unknown |
| V3-UIX-03 | Provide display for empty state, loading, error, no-permission, and 409-conflict reasons across every path (four states — ok/loading/empty/error — on every screen/every UI component). Never show a raw error string; map error codes to Japanese plus a generic fallback, and for stale data such as an unsaved photo, show an honest empty state ("No photo (not saved at commit time)") rather than a misleading placeholder. | Non-Functional Requirement | High | — | Wave 1 | Implemented |
#### 5.2.7 Cost and Operational Transparency (5 items)

| ID | Requirement Text | Type | trust | Human Confirmation | wave | ver2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V3-CST-01 | Prioritize a structure that can sustain costs even if the user base does not grow over 10 years; absolutely avoid usage-based fees that scale with the number of users (variable costs such as embedding API calls), and keep compute resources on the user side. Target operating cost is in the range of 300–1000 yen/month, centered on serverless (Cloudflare Pages/Workers/R2) plus a minimal VPS (only for email/magic-link and webhook relay), aiming for zero server cost, automatic scaling, no maintenance, and zero operational manpower ("a civilization run just by plugging in a USB"). Read-only functions (e.g. search) require no account and are load-balanced, while only write functions require authentication; maintenance processing is batched (every 5 minutes / 1 hour / 1 day). DDL constraints are ported into the kernel so the system runs on R2 + POST alone. | 非機能要件 | 中 | — | 第1波 | 部分 |
| V3-CST-04 | Select the cheapest infrastructure: adopt a custom domain with the lowest annual renewal fee TLD (cheapest .uk), consolidate DNS, R2, and email forwarding into a single Cloudflare account, run the API on the cheapest always-on VPS (Sakura 512MB, Rocky Linux), and reuse the existing R2 key if its scope covers the target bucket. For storage/DB, prioritize minimizing cost over 10 years, including configurations cheaper than R2 (e.g., Backblaze B2 + Cloudflare CDN with free egress), leveraging each option's strengths to minimize cost overall. | 制約 | 中 | — | 第1波 | 不明 |
| V3-CST-06 | Keep generative AI costs down: for materials with high generation cost such as BGM, do not auto-generate — have AI only select from existing free material. Before image generation, always run a RAG (past-generation) search first, and reuse an existing image if one exists above a similarity threshold (around 0.75); generate new images only when none is found. | 非機能要件 | 中 | — | 対象外 | 不明 |
| V3-CST-07 | Operate the business using a GMO Aozora Bank sole-proprietor account plus a credit card to keep receipts organized, running debt-free at low running cost. The API is opened free of charge for research purposes only; commercial use is prohibited. Set the seller's take higher than platforms like Yahoo Auctions (i.e., lower fees), and use dynamic data (ongoing observation, transaction history) to make copying data to competitors meaningless. | 制約 | 中 | — | 対象外 | 不明 |
| V3-CST-08 | As a solo-developer premise, keep operations lightweight: Gantt charts, kanban, CI/CD, team-sharing features, and schedule estimates are unnecessary (solo, no team, no deadlines). The dev machine's drive layout is C = Windows only / D = development & AI (fast) / F = games / E = bulk storage, and unify the default install location for development tools under D:\Programs. | 非機能要件 | 中 | — | 対象外 | 不明 |

| V3-AIP-03 | Always pass implementation artifacts through an independent critic/audit agent (the implementing EXEC and the auditing AUDIT must be separate agents) before delivery. Rubber-stamping/checkbox theater is prohibited; PASS is granted only when accompanied by real browser verification, and completion marks are attached only when there is measured evidence. Never claim a non-functioning feature "works." | 制約 | 高 | — | 第1波 | 実装済み |

Also maintain a mechanism to mock locally in memory (without swapping out production code, R2) so the entire backend can be integration-tested. E2E treats CI (Ubuntu) as authoritative to absorb local OS differences. | 非機能要件 | 高 | — | 第1波 | 部分 |

| V3-FND-10 | In ver4, avoid concentrating load unevenly: place the main API, scaling, and R2 bindings on Cloudflare Workers, and keep the Sakura VPS (512MB) as a thin, always-on process doing only SMTP-based magic-link sending + lightweight kicks. Do not put Next.js, heavy DINOv2 batch processing, or server-side SwitchBot polling on the VPS. Heavy ML inference and SwitchBot polling (holding token+secret) use the user's PC collector/import/manual entry as the master path. ver3, which loads the entire FastAPI stack onto the VPS, is a provisional compromise configuration (expiring on completion of the ver4 migration) and must not be mistaken for the final form. | 制約 | 高 | — | 第1波 | 部分 |

CORS headers must be consistently attached to all responses, including 500/502; Pages builds must run every route as runtime=edge, output to .vercel/output/static, and delete .next/cache (avoiding the 25MiB overage), and the reverse proxy must not strip JSON responses. | 制約 | 高 | — | 第1波 | 実装済み |

## 5.3 Layer-Separation ADR (Truth = Immutable, Projection = Regenerable)

> **ADR-V3-LAYER-01 — Separation of the Truth Layer (append-only, immutable) and the Projection Layer (regenerable)**
> Status: Proposed (connects to the DIFF-C-26 layer-separation ruling; an ADR bundling the 42 constraints in §5.2.1 and CL-01/02/05/12/13 in §5.4)
> Date: 2026-07-10

### Context

ver3-live is already operating with "writes to R2 are INSERT ONLY, with UPDATE, DELETE, and key overwrite prohibited (no-overwrite = re-put on the same key returns 409)" (V3-FND-01). The permanent master is Cloudflare R2 alone; a resident DB (Postgres/SQLite/Qdrant/pgvector, etc.) is never the source of truth (V3-FND-02). Meanwhile, read requests such as search, listing, similarity, and aggregation cannot be satisfied by R2's directory structure alone (V3-FND-03). If an "append-only event sequence that, once written, cannot be deleted" and a "structure for fast reads" are mixed into the same storage and the same responsibility here, schema changes will break the identity of the existing Truth (Genesis Hash + hash chain, V3-FND-05).

### Decision

Separate the system into 2 layers.

| Layer | Definition | Mutability | Concrete Instances | Corresponding Requirements |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Truth Layer** | An append-only event sequence of facts that occurred. The source of the civilization's identity | **Immutable** (INSERT ONLY / no-overwrite / UPDATE and DELETE prohibited / R2 tokens have no delete permission) | R2 event/snapshot, consent records, karma/platinum ledger, tag events, collector-signed environment POSTs | V3-FND-01 / 05 / 06, V3-KRM-19, V3-OBS-63 |
| **Projection Layer** | Read-oriented structures regenerated from Truth via pure functions. A cache for speed and search | **Regenerable** (can be discarded at any time and restored from Truth; not the master) | Parquet manifest, materialized views, embedding generation, latest.json pointer, aggregates on DuckDB/Polars | V3-FND-03 / 06, V3-FND-04 (Reducer), V3-BBS-05 |

**Invariant**: The projection layer is a function of the Truth layer, `projection = f(truth_events)`, where `f` is deterministic (same input → same output, zero side effects, V3-FND-04). Even if the projection is completely erased, re-running `f` from Truth must always guarantee full restoration. Conversely, facts that exist only in the projection layer must never be created (a projection does not generate facts).

### Consequences

- **Positive consequences**: (1) Schema evolution can be absorbed by swapping the projection layer's `f`, without touching Truth's byte sequence. (2) Failure recovery simplifies to a single move: "discard the projection and regenerate from Truth." (3) Auditing closes with hash-chain verification at the Truth layer.
- **Costs**: (1) Every read-optimization incurs the cost of regenerating `f` (low latency is accepted as a non-requirement, V3-FND-03). (2) Projection freshness is subordinate to the timing of pointer updates.
- **Frozen scope**: CL-01/CL-02/CL-05/CL-12/CL-13 in §5.4 (the 5 core append-only layers) have their **event schema formally frozen** until this ADR is finalized. Changes are made only on the projection-layer side.

### Rejected Alternatives

- **Making a resident DB the master**: SQL/JOINs become easier, but this would abandon V3-FND-02 (R2 as the sole master) and no-overwrite auditability, so it is rejected. A DB is permitted only as a cache within the projection layer.
- **Making Truth mutable and overwriting the "latest value"**: Reads would be fast, but the Genesis Hash chain (V3-FND-05) would no longer hold, losing the civilization's identity and tamper-detection, so it is rejected.

## 5.4 The 13 Compatibility-Mandatory Layers (Input to the Phase B Migration Strategy)

We tabulate the 13 layers that **must remain compatible** with the current production system (ver3-live), as input to the migration strategy. The criteria are "RTM status = implemented/partially implemented" AND "touches production's persistent data format, authentication boundary, existing user identifiers, or existing signing/derivation code" (master: `ver3-要件定義抽出書-v2` §6.4). These form the subset of Chapter 6's RTM mapping that is **absorbed and format-compatibility-mandatory**, showing concretely which production assets this chapter's constraint set (especially §5.2.1 and the §5.3 ADR) protects.

| # | Compatibility-Mandatory Layer | RTM Basis (doc / item) | V3 ID | Why It Cannot Be Broken |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CL-01 | R2 INSERT ONLY / no-overwrite (event schema) | 00-Foundation FOUND-D02/D05/D06, 05-Observation OBS-R2-01–05, 15-Data Design FR-DATA-03/04/06 | V3-FND-01 | The production event_store is already running append-only. Breaking the R2 token constraint of no key overwrite / no delete permission would destroy the identity of the existing Truth. |
| CL-02 | Truth provenance metadata (run_id/schema_version/value_origin) | 00-Foundation FOUND-D07/D08, 15-Data Design FR-DATA-09–12 | V3-FND-15 / V3-FND-18 / V3-PPR-09 | The field contract of reproducibility metadata already attached to existing records. A schema change breaks existing replay. |
| CL-03 | actor_id resolution / session | 01-Login FR-LOGIN-06/08 (session, resolve_actor_id) | V3-AUT-17 / V3-AUT-19 | The actor_id derivation logic for existing users. Changing it breaks the attribution of existing events (*the session scheme JWT vs. opaque is ruled separately in V3-AUT-03). |
| CL-04 | Authentication boundary, Scope A public READ (deny-by-default) | 01-Login FR-LOGIN-07, 05-Observation OBS-GAP-01–03 | V3-AUT-15 / V3-AUT-16 / V3-OBS-39 | The unauthenticated public scope of observation READ is live in production. Narrowing or widening the boundary breaks the existing public/protected contract. |
| CL-05 | Terms-of-service consent records (append-only, separate file) | 02-Terms of Service FR-13/14/21, NFR-05 | V3-SEC-20 / V3-SEC-23 / V3-FND-01 | Legal consent events are held INSERT ONLY. The multiple-consents = separate-file scheme is legal evidence, so its format cannot be changed. |
| CL-06 | Individual keys individual_id / sire_id / dam_id | 00-Product Policy FR-MVP-04 | V3-IND-01 | The parent-child linkage key for existing observation data. Changing the naming/granularity makes existing individuals/pedigrees unreferenceable. |
| CL-07 | Observation R2 image + thumbnail contract (long edge 512px JPEG, EXIF transpose) | 18-Photo Analysis FR-18-06 | V3-OBS-23 / V3-OBS-08 | The generation contract for blobs and thumbnails already stored in production. A spec change breaks existing image URLs/display. |
| CL-08 | Embedding generation contract (dinov2, L2 normalization, append-only) | 18-Photo Analysis FR-18-07 | V3-OBS-09 | If dimensionality/normalization is incompatible with the existing embedding vectors, similarity search fails entirely (dimension 384/768 must be confirmed first via DIFF-C-18). |
| CL-09 | Collector Ed25519 signature + non-exposure of secrets | 13-Data Sources FR-ENV-06/07/08 | V3-OBS-17 / V3-SEC-02 / V3-OBS-29 | The live collector protocol. Changing the signing key/verification causes environment POSTs from existing collectors to be rejected entirely. |
| CL-10 | Individual QR token issuance, scanning, and observation resumption | 00-Product Policy FR-MVP-05, 13-Data Sources FR-ENV-05 | V3-OBS-20 / V3-SEC-44 | Already-issued QR codes (app-scheme deep links) are circulating as physical labels. A token-format change invalidates existing QRs. |
| CL-11 | GMO deriveTransferCode(userId) transfer code | 23-GMO FR-GMO-01/05, NFR-GMO-01 | V3-MKT-12 / V3-MKT-14 | The transfer code (SHA-256 → Base36 → U-XXXX) already derived for existing users. Changing the derivation function breaks deposit reconciliation. |
| CL-12 | Karma / platinum ledger, append-only | 08-Karma FR-KRM-09, 14-Contribution NFR-CONTRIB-01, 22-PT Market FR-PTMKT-03 | V3-KRM-19 / V3-FND-01 | All existing mutations are already recorded R2 INSERT ONLY. A ledger-format change would throw off balance/trust recalculation. |
| CL-13 | Tag append-only events + aggregate view | 15-Data Design FR-DATA-15 | V3-OBS-63 / V3-BBS-13 | Existing tag events are already append-only tag_event. Changing the aggregate-view assumptions makes existing tag aggregation inconsistent. |

**Implications for the migration strategy**: CL-01/CL-02/CL-05/CL-12/CL-13 are the **core of the append-only principle** and are formally frozen until §5.3 ADR-V3-LAYER-01 is finalized. CL-06/CL-08/CL-10/CL-11 are **identifiers tied to existing users/physical items**, whose format cannot be changed without a backward-compatible conversion table. CL-03/CL-04/CL-09 are **authentication and boundaries**, linked to the session-scheme (V3-AUT-03) and role-system (V3-AUT-22) rulings. In Phase B, these 13 layers are split into "frozen (format maintained)" and "requires conversion bridge," and the migration order is organized around this split.

# Chapter 6 RTM Integration (Requirements Traceability)

## 6.0 Positioning

This chapter integrates the results of mapping the ver2 requirements traceability matrix (RTM, 167 items) in full to the ver3 requirements, and declares that **the requirement IDs in this document (the final requirements specification) are the master rows of the new RTM**. The primary data for the mapping is `rtm-mapping-final.json` (appendix `ver3-rtm-mapping-v1.csv`), which is the source of truth.

## 6.1 RTM 167 → V3 Coverage Summary

The following shows the ver2 RTM's 167 items classified by type (absorbed / changed / dropped / missing).

| Type | Count | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| absorbed | 163 | Absorbed into a ver3 entry (of which 3 newly added items resolved missing cases) |
| changed | 2 | Concept retained but implementation approach changed in ver3 |
| dropped | 2 | Out of scope for ver3 (explicitly not adopted) |
| missing | 0 | Unaddressed (originally 3 items, reduced to 0 by backfilling new V3 entries) |
| **Total** | **167** | — |

**Reconciliation**: 163 + 2 + 2 + 0 = **167** (matches the RTM total). This is also consistent with the ver2 RTM breakdown by status (implemented 57 / partial 29 / unimplemented 41 / human gate 30 / out of scope 10 = 167). "Unimplemented" is the vocabulary of the original RTM and corresponds to "not started" in §2 of this document.

**changed (2 items)** — Concept inherited but implementation approach changed.

| RTM Item | V3 ID | Change Content |

| 07/19 file-board linkage (1 master MD = 1 thread, registry sync) | V3-AIP-37 / V3-AIP-93 / V3-BBS-26 | Linkage originating from civ-os C-Sync changed to unify on GitHub (AIP-37 C-Sync fully not adopted). The linkage concept remains in AIP-93 |

**dropped (2 items)** — Explicitly out of scope for ver3.

| RTM Item | V3 ID | Reason for Exclusion |
|---|---|---|
| 04 H-060/H-061 first-time Twin tour | V3-UIX-65 | Explicitly decided not to adopt the Twin persona in the IHL UI |
| 15 Full maintenance of the cross-cutting unified data catalog MD (fb_00096) | (none) | Explicitly out of scope in the RTM. V3 adopts parquet manifest search (V3-FND-03) and does not treat a unified catalog MD as a requirement |

**missing → newly backfilled (3 items)** — 3 items for which the RTM had no corresponding V3 entry were backfilled with new entries and marked absorbed (resulting in missing = 0). These 3 items hold their master ID for the first time in this document.

| RTM Item | New V3 ID | Reason for Backfill |
|---|---|---|
| 07 FR-BBS-07 post rescue (hiding raw errors, retry, save boundary) | **V3-BBS-35** | The mechanism to preserve board post content was missing from the existing V3 set |

| 12 FR-SET-19 completion nudge for pre-transaction settings | **V3-UIX-80** | The completion nudge / incomplete-state guidance was missing |

## 6.3 Convention Making This Document's Requirement IDs the Master Rows of the New RTM

- **Switching the master**: From ver3 onward, the primary key for traceability shall be **this document's V3-* requirement IDs** (the functional requirements in Chapters 1–4 and each row of the full table in §5.2), not the ver2 RTM item IDs (FR-xxx / NFR-xxx). The ver2 RTM item IDs remain only as a "migration-source reference" column. **However, rows in §5.2 tagged with a pending/rejected badge (【Pending — see Chapter 7】/ rejected constraint) are excluded from the master rows of the new RTM and are incorporated only after the ruling in Chapter 7 is finalized.**
- **Direction of mapping**: The mapping from old RTM item → V3 ID is many-to-many (both cases occur: one RTM item decomposed into multiple V3 entries, and multiple RTM items consolidated into one V3 entry). `rtm-mapping-final.json` is the source of truth for this mapping. The reverse lookup (V3 ID → originating RTM) is mechanically generated from the same file.
- **Structure of new RTM rows**: One row of the new RTM = `{V3 ID, requirement text, kind, wave, originating RTM item (optional), corresponding TC (assigned in Phase C), implementation commit (Phase D onward)}`. The requirement text, kind, and wave are sourced from the tables in §5.2 and Chapters 1–4 of this document.
- **Frozen scope**: For V3 ID rows corresponding to the 13 mandatory-compatibility layers (CL-01–13) in §5.4, the event schema columns are frozen until the corresponding TC turns green (per §5.3 ADR).

## 6.4 Phase C Requirement → Test Case Table Generation Convention

In Phase C, a **test case (TC) table** is generated from each V3 requirement ID in this document, filling the `corresponding TC` column of the new RTM. The convention for connecting to the acceptance criterion "80% test green" is defined below.

- **Generation unit**: At least 1 TC per requirement ID. Constraints (`kind=constraint`) must include at least 1 "negative TC that fails when violated" (e.g., V3-FND-01 → a TC where a re-put with the same key returns 409). Non-functional requirements are reduced to measurable-threshold TCs.
- **TC table columns**: `{TC-ID, corresponding V3 ID, type (positive/negative/nonfunc), preconditions, steps, expected result, automatable (yes/no), status (red/green/blocked)}`.
- **Connection to acceptance criteria**: Acceptance for each wave is judged by "**80% or more of the automatable TCs** tied to the requirement IDs belonging to that wave are green." TCs where `automatable=no` (human gates such as physical fixtures, video publication, or production keys) are excluded from the denominator and referred to human-gate judgment (the maintenance gates in §7.3: money/legal/production keys/video publication/physical fixtures).
- **Evidence for green**: A green mark is granted only when there is measured evidence (test execution logs, real-browser verification) (per the V3-AIP-03 critic gate; "do not say a non-working feature 'works'"). Rubber-stamping is prohibited.
- **Priority for mandatory-compatibility layers**: Negative TCs (detecting breakage of format compatibility) for requirement IDs corresponding to CL-01–13 in §5.4 are greened with **top priority in Phase C**, and subsequent implementation must not break this TC as a regression condition.
- **Coverage calculation**: "Requirement coverage" is separately calculated with the total number of requirement IDs as the denominator and the number of requirement IDs with at least 1 corresponding TC as the numerator (visualizing requirements with no TC generated yet). The 80% green (TC-level) and requirement coverage (requirement-level) metrics are reported separately.

## 7.3 Rejected (Subject to the Withdrawal Ledger) — 2 Items

R2 applies. These are in a withdrawn direction, and reviving them requires explicit approval.

| ID | Reason for Rejection | Round 3 Ruling (rejection maintained + addendum) |
|---|---|---|
| `V3-UIX-13` | Falls under R-9. The Builder's unified-IDE-ification across 15 edit modes was narrowed by the individual's own design-aesthetics ruling (H-01=B, 2026-07-05 "leave feature development to GitHub"). Since this is not a rejection due to insufficient technology, it is outside the scope of "revive if technology is insufficient." | Confirmed by the user ("That is correct"). Addendum: "Instead, the Builder should be made easy to use as a no-code UIBuilder that anyone can use to edit screens" — noted as a design condition for V3-UIX-61 / V3-UIX-09. |
| `V3-SEC-32` | Falls under R-7. "The core (DB physical structure/internal karma computation/dispute/PT economy/history) is permanently non-public" was converted to fully public on 2026-06-30 with "it's fine to open-source it on GitHub." | Confirmed by the user ("That is correct. There's no point hoarding it alone. It's important to separate responsibilities and componentize so that only the parts people want to use for OSS can be cut out and used. Maintainability also improves, and like metabolism, the system won't rot and can keep up with the times") — noted as a reinforcing source text for Thought ② (fork culture). |

## 7.4 Out of System Requirement Scope (Kept as Reference)

Items respected as desires/philosophy but not reflected in ver3 system requirements. Recorded for reference only.

| ID | Treatment |
|---|---|
| `V3-FND-26` | A P2P/IPFS/libp2p distributed architecture was considered and ultimately not adopted, in order to preserve single-node determinism. |
| `V3-VID-25` | The method for the transmission hook (collaboration/borrowing a format) is a planning hint. A reference memo, not a system requirement. |
| `V3-OTH-26` | A home structure fully cleanable by a robot vacuum is a lifestyle example oriented toward "minimal maintenance cost." Not reflected in requirements. |
| `V3-FND-29` | Physical design of the breeding facility (full compatibility with robot vacuum cleaners, no floor-level wiring, zero-step thresholds, leg height 0/18cm+). Confirmed out of scope by the Round 3 ruling ("Out of scope"). Not carried into system requirements; kept as reference only. |

# Chapter 8 Acceptance Criteria and Verification

## 8.1 Correspondence with intent acceptance_criteria

Maps the acceptance criteria of intent step5 to their fulfillment in this requirements specification (and subsequent phases).

| intent Criterion | Fulfillment Location | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Extraction document consistent with the RTM | This document's §RTM integration edition (167/167 mapped in Phase A) | Fulfilled |
| Auto-generated test cases for major use cases, 80% green | This document finalizes through the "requirement → TC table." The green determination is carried forward to the implementation phase (Phase C) | Fulfilled on the design side; judged during implementation |
| Development plan with milestones and 3-point estimates | B3 development plan (optimistic/median/pessimistic) | Fulfilled in B3 |
| Video: automation design for script → video → posting | B5 video mass-production pipeline design | Fulfilled in B5 |
| 5 or more deep-research sources as basis | Mandatory for each B2 selection | Fulfilled in B2 |

## 8.2 Critic Gate Convention

Codifies intent's invariant principle that "nothing that fails to pass the critic is delivered."

| Item | Convention |
|---|---|
| Role separation | The implementing/drafting EXEC and the auditing AUDIT must always be separate agents. Self-approval by the same agent is prohibited. |
| Preceding deterministic checks | Deterministic checks such as lint/test/schema must always pass before submission to the critic (V3-AIP-04). |
| 4-point critique | Each deliverable is subjected to "spec-conformance critique / source-existence check / intent-coverage check / contradiction check." |
| PASS condition | When real-browser verification is involved, PASS is granted only on the basis of that measured result. A completion mark is attached only when there is measured evidence. |
| Prohibited | Rubber-stamping / checkbox theater. Do not say a non-working feature "works." |
| Round-trip governance | An upper limit is set on critic round-trips; upon reaching the limit, finishing falls back to the planning side, and the round-trip log is fully recorded in markdown. |
| Specific to this document | RTM coverage of 100% is mechanically reconciled. Design documents are cross-checked for mutual consistency across the 3 types × 2 languages. |

## 8.3 Revision Convention for This Document

| Item | Convention |
|---|---|
| Version | This document is v1. Subsequent revisions are numbered semver-style (v1 → v1.1 → v2), with changes appended at the front. |
| Master language | **The Japanese edition is the sole master.** In case of conflict, Japanese takes precedence. |
| English edition | A separate file with identical content (e.g., `final-requirements.en.md`). Treated as an agent-auto-generated derivative, synced by batch generation whenever the Japanese edition is finalized (no manual dual maintenance — V3-I18-16). |
| Reflection | Finalization of pending items and ruling changes are reflected in both the relevant section of this document and the ruling record, and the statistics are updated. |
| Commit governance | Every commit includes an "autonomous execution rationale" and a "referenced report ID." `.env` and secret keys must never be committed. |
